This spec owns the items that change existing code.\n\n## Goal & Context\n\nThe model workspace (~86k lines of Rust) hand-rolls a few things that admitted or widely used crates do, and repeats several small helpers across crates. This spec replaces those with crates or one shared copy, moves hand-rolled golden comparisons onto insta, and drops the binary-snapshot layout that forced the fixture runner to decode insta's private file naming. Checker semantics, Model meaning and the semantic protocol contract are preserved; internal and wire representations may change with coordinated regeneration. Do not add legacy decoders, dual formats or old-guest support. Verify Model meaning, the current native contract, typed replay, reproducibility and safety instead. Deterministic ordering is a requirement of the new contract, not an obligation to retain old bytes. Pinned tool checksums, content-addressed integrity and actual proofs/negative controls remain required.\n\n## Architecture & Data Models\n\n- **Crates (R1).** `heck` replaces three hand-written snake/camel converters, so generated names follow prost-build by construction. `darling` (admitted under fn-210 R18, unused so far) parses the flag, `key = value` and rename parts of catalog `ApiAttributes` and `proposal_handler`; source-ordered tag/constraint families, positional `also(uuid, ...)`, ordered `oneof(...)`, `rejects(...)` and the rule/mirror split stay hand-written. `thiserror` replaces hand-written `Display`+`Error` impls in umpire-catalog and umpire-dsl where it reads cleaner. Prost-build's default WKT mappings replace in-house well-known-type generation consistently across runtime, Kettle and shared Temporal codegen: nonprimitive types use `prost-types`, Empty uses unit and scalar/string/bytes wrappers use the corresponding Rust primitives; descriptor-derived paths use the same mapping. A thin wrapper keeps the Malformed-versus-Unsupported refusal split of `any.rs`. Recursion-only matches use them: `Expr::infallible` (only its non-recursive `false` arms stay explicit), `umpire-tla` `names::rename` and `emit::prime`, the fixtures' interpreted `rewrite`. The fixture runner (`umpire/dev/testing/src/runner.rs`) moves from `assert_binary_snapshot!` (metadata `.snap` plus payload `.snap.<ext>`) to text snapshots, one `<name>.snap` per golden: the logical name is the path minus `.snap`, so `logical_file`/`golden_files` shrink to a suffix strip; consumer ownership and the incomplete/pending checks for skipped backends stay. Readers of payload files (`include_str!` of `.snap.txt` in other crates and guests, Go tests reading sibling goldens) read through one small helper that strips the insta header, or assert their own snapshot.\n\n## API Contracts\n\n- umpire-checker: `pub fn panic_message(payload: &(dyn Any + Send)) -> &str`.\n- umpire-ir: `ExprKind::children(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &Expr>`, `ExprKind::children_mut(&mut self) -> impl Iterator<Item = &mut Expr>`.\n- umpire-catalog: one public applicability mapping returning `ProbeApplicability`.\n- umpire-testing: `replace_once`, `replace_in`, `replace_code` (signatures from the current copies), and a golden-reading helper returning snapshot contents without the insta header.\n- No Model-surface change. Protocol representations (`umpire.model.v1`, `umpire.run.v1`), generated types and their Rust/Go readers may be updated where required by the planned crate and snapshot consolidation. Cover non-fixture named snapshots with explicit live-test ownership too; no blanket ignore list or unchecked snapshot subtree. Errors: after the move no `model2` path remains outside history, and `make model-check` passes.\n88:- **R44:** (2026-10-18, owner approved; amended 2026-10-19 to the flat form after the nested and marker-trait forms were judged too much ceremony) A Model is one inline `mod` carrying `#[machine]` (`kettle::Model`), holding the `#[derive(State)]` struct, the `#[derive(Action)]` enums and one inherent `impl Model` whose methods carry per-item kind attributes: `#[on(Actor)]` (one `match` method per actor, match guards as the guards), `#[effect]` (optional helpers that may take `&mut self`), `#[invariant]`, `#[postcondition]` / `#[postcondition(after = Actor::Action)]`, `#[assumption]`, `#[progress]`. `#[machine]` enforces grouping and order: within the impl, items of one kind are contiguous and the kinds appear in that order; the check is a hard error at the first out-of-place item naming the kind it must follow and precede; unattributed helper methods are ignored by the order check but must not take `&mut self` (only `#[on]` and `#[effect]` may). Catalog order is the `Action` enums' declaration order; `#[machine]` takes no arguments and emits `#[forbid(unsafe_code)]` on the module. Every attribute is pass-through (R36); nothing is rewritten.
